## PM: consolidation

_Reaped 1 background process(es): 1 term_

### Verdict

`approved`: UX Lead and UI Lead validated the flow and the screens. Both jobs can be completed and every failure has a recovery, so no finding blocks a job; what remains is friction and goes to the PR as observations.

### PRD

#### Problem and job

**Job story.** When I have recorded a sale, or a customer later asks what they took or what they owe, I want to send them a short plain-text bill of that sale in one tap, so I can answer in our chat without retyping or working out figures, and the customer sees the same numbers I do.

**When the seller uses it:**
- **Main moment:** right after saving, while the customer is still waiting in the chat.
- **Second moment:** later, when the customer asks. The seller opens that sale's detail and taps the same button.

**Today (assumption):** the seller retypes the order and the total, or sends a screenshot, and works out the balance by hand. A different answer would not change what this PRD defines.

The story says "the chat" and names no channel (Assume nothing).

#### Scope

- The sale's detail gets a Share/Copy button that sends that sale's **Sale bill**. The button is never inside the "Sale saved" notice.
- The bill is plain text and holds only facts. It is not an invoice; invoices stay out of scope in the brief.
- A payment receipt is not part of this job.
- Nothing about the bill is stored.

#### Success criteria

1. **One tap, no typing.**
   - On the phone, one tap either opens the phone's share sheet with the bill ready or puts the bill on the clipboard.
   - Choosing the chat and sending are the phone's own steps.
   - If sharing or copying fails, the seller still gets the text without typing.
2. **The figures match the app.**
   - The bill's total, payment state, still-owed amount and balance always agree with the sale's detail.
   - They come from the same settlement pass as the detail's "still owing", in the same response. Nothing on the front works settlement out again.
   - **Evidence:** a reference set of bills. Its expected texts are written by hand against this PRD, never generated, and the set must show 0 mismatches. It covers:
     - with and without a bulk line;
     - a VIP line and a special line, both reading like list lines;
     - a gift line;
     - a $0 sale of gifts only, with a balance that is not zero;
     - paid in cash, paid by transfer, paid later, partly owed, fully owed;
     - a balance above $0, a credit, and a balance of exactly $0;
     - a sale with no customer;
     - with and without shipping;
     - one line and several lines;
     - quantities 0,01 · 0,1 · 1,05 · 1.000.
3. **Nothing private leaks.**
   - The bill has no note, no customer name, no VIP, special or list wording, no ids and no links.
   - A VIP line or a special-price line reads like a list line.
   - **Evidence:** the leak test, which also checks that the text has no `*`, `_` or backtick.
4. **Assume nothing holds.**
   - Every fixed word and the unit come from the copy module.
   - The unit is one copy entry, "g" by default, until #318.
   - **Evidence:** with the unit set to "u", the bill reads "3,5 u" and "/u". A guard also checks that no sale bill source holds a "g" literal or calls the grams formatter.

#### The bill text

In this order:
1. **Header:** the business name, then the date as dd/mm on a new line, with no year. A blank line follows.
2. **One line per sale line:**
   - a list, VIP or special line: `{product} {quantity} {unit} · {amount}`
   - a bulk line: `{product} {quantity} {unit} · bulk price {unitPrice}/{unit} · {amount}`
   - a gift: `{product} {quantity} {unit} · Gift · $ 0`
3. **Shipping:** `Shipping · {amount}`, only when shipping charged is above $0.
4. **Total:** a blank line, then `Total · {amount}`.
5. **Payment line:**
   - "Paid in cash" or "Paid by transfer" for an on-the-spot payment;
   - "Paid" when the sale was paid in full later;
   - `Still owed on this sale: {amount}` when something is still owed on it, partly or fully;
   - no line when the payment state is none.
6. **Balance line:**
   - `Your balance: {amount}` when the balance is above $0;
   - `In your favor: {amount}` for a credit, shown as its magnitude.

**Formats:**
- Quantities are worked out from whole hundredths, never from a float. They use Argentine separators and drop trailing zeros: 0,01 · 0,1 · 1,05 · 3,5 · 1.000.
- The app's existing grams display reads exactly as it does today.
- Money reads `$ 11.000`, never in the catalog's format.

**Lines that can be missing:**
- Shipping, when it is $0.
- The payment line, when the payment state is none.
- The balance line, when the sale has no customer or the balance is exactly $0.

The balance is the customer's whole account at the moment of sharing, not on the sale's date. For a sale shared later the two can differ, and this is intended.

#### Flow: send the bill right after saving (main moment)

**Start:** the seller has just saved a sale. Per `sales.md`, the app opens the sale's detail (`VentaDetalle`) with the "Sale saved" toast, which has Undo. The customer is waiting in the chat.

**When the phone can share (the usual case):**
1. On `VentaDetalle`, the seller taps Share (touch 1). The button is part of the screen's content, outside the toast.
2. The phone's share sheet opens with the bill ready.
3. The seller picks the chat and sends. These are the phone's own steps.
4. The seller is back on `VentaDetalle` and nothing is shown. A cancelled share also shows nothing.

**When the phone can't share (plain http, or no share support):**
1. The button reads Copy. The seller taps it (touch 1).
2. The toast "Bill copied" shows.
3. The seller switches to the chat and pastes.

**Touch count:** 1 in the app with either label, and no typing.

**Failures and how the seller recovers:**
- **Share fails.** The attention icon and "Couldn't share. Tap Copy and paste it into the chat." show, and the label flips to Copy. One more tap on Copy (2 in total) brings "Bill copied". The seller then pastes.
- **Copy fails.** "Couldn't copy. Select the text below and copy it by hand." shows, with the bill in a selectable "Sale bill" block. The seller selects and copies it with the phone's own gestures, still without typing.
- **Share cancelled.** Nothing shows. The seller can tap again.
- **Loading.** The button is disabled and busy. The seller waits; nothing else is needed.
- **No bill.** The button is hidden and the rest of the detail still shows.
- **Not found.** The existing not-found path applies.

**End:** the seller is on `VentaDetalle`, with the "Sale saved" toast and its Undo as the product draws them.

#### Flow: send the bill later, when the customer asks

1. On Sales, the seller taps the sale's row (touch 1).
2. On `VentaDetalle`, they tap Share or Copy (touch 2).
3. The outcomes and recoveries are the same as in the main flow.

**Touch count:** 2 from Sales, or 3 from Home or another tab (tab, row, button). A failed share adds 1 (Copy).

**Notes:**
- The balance line is the customer's account now.
- A sale with no customer keeps the button, and its bill has no balance line.
- Coming back to the screen refetches the detail, so the text is always built from the latest detail.
- The back stays "‹ Sales". The button adds no route.

#### Flow: the detail's other uses

Viewing the detail, "Delete sale" with its confirmation, and Undo in "Sale saved" keep their steps and touch counts. The new button must not push "Delete sale" out of reach.

#### Flow: computer

The computer has the same job in `VentaDetalleCompu`, with the same counts as clicks. It gets no dedicated polish (Phone first).

#### Screen: VentaDetalle (phone)

**Kept as drawn** in `VentaDetalle.dc.html`:
- the back "‹ Sales" (the only back);
- the title "Sale of dd/mm" and the amber total card;
- "Lines" with their rule chips;
- "Shipping charged" and "Total";
- "Delete sale" and the bar with Sales current.

**Share/Copy button**
- **Placement:** the first item of the bottom action stack, above "Delete sale". The stack is `margin-top: auto`, `padding: 16px 24px 24px`, `gap: 10px`. The button sits in the screen's content, never inside the "Sale saved" toast.
- **Form:**
  - icon and text, because this is the screen's main action, so the icon-only exemption does not apply;
  - the existing filled primary style, like "Save the sale" on NuevaVenta;
  - 48 px high and full width, and not styled as a link;
  - a decorative leading icon (`aria-hidden`, 22 px, stroke 1.6): `compartir` for Share and `copiar` for Copy, both existing `NombreIcono` names;
  - the visible text and the accessible name are the word alone, "Share" or "Copy".
- **Touch:** at least 44 px; tokens only; `user-select: none`; `touch-action: manipulation`; an `:active` state like the other primary buttons.
- **"Delete sale"** keeps its quiet text-and-icon style below the button.
- **Small screens:** at 320 px with text at 130 %, the content scrolls above the stack, and both actions stay reachable.

**States**

| State | What shows |
|---|---|
| Filled, can share | "Share" with `compartir`, enabled. |
| Filled, can't share | "Copy" with `copiar`, enabled. |
| Loading | The label is kept, with the existing disabled style and `aria-busy="true"`. No digits and no `$`. The screen's loading skeleton stands in for the figures. |
| Copied | The existing toast (`Estados`, "Avisos al guardar"), "Bill copied", with no icon and no action. |
| Share cancelled | Nothing changes. |
| Share failed | An existing notice with the attention icon (`atencion`), "Couldn't share. Tap Copy and paste it into the chat.", directly above the button in the stack. The label flips to "Copy" with `copiar`. |
| Copy failed | The notice "Couldn't copy. Select the text below and copy it by hand." in the same position, plus a selectable "Sale bill" block holding the bill text. |
| No bill (`bill: null`) | The button is absent, and the stack holds "Delete sale" only. No message is shown. |
| Not found | The existing not-found screen. |
| "Sale saved" | The toast with Undo, unchanged, with the button outside it. |

**Selectable block "Sale bill"**
- Built from the existing card surface and body type the "Lines" rows use, with token radius and padding.
- `white-space: pre-wrap`, so line breaks are kept, and `user-select: text`.
- Accessible name "Sale bill".
- It appears only after a failed copy and is the only place the bill text shows in the app.
- As designed, it sits between the totals and the action stack.

#### Screen: VentaDetalleCompu (computer)

Everything already drawn is kept. The button, its states, the toast, the failure notices and the block use the same components and the same position: at the foot of the detail's column, above "Delete sale". The computer gets no dedicated polish.

#### Button behaviour (rules every state follows)

- **Label:** Share where the phone can share; otherwise Copy.
- **Feedback:**
  - A successful share shows nothing; the sheet closing is the confirmation.
  - A successful copy shows the toast "Bill copied".
- **No preview:** the text shows on screen only when copying fails.
- **No bill:**
  - If the server can't build a bill, it gives none. The rest of the sale's detail still shows and the button is hidden.
  - A sale with no customer keeps the button.
- **Fresh text:** always built from the latest detail the screen holds. The detail refreshes as it does today.
- **Plain http:**
  - The share or copy call runs right inside the tap, with nothing awaited before it (ADR-0002).
  - It works over the home network on plain http.
  - It passes the mobile check with no new offender.
  - `WalkState` gains the copy-failed and loading states.
- **Silent:** nothing is written to the console or to storage.

#### Out of scope

- A year in the date.
- A preview step.
- An "already shared" state.
- A payment receipt.
- Choosing a channel, or sending from the app.
- Making the unit customizable (#318).
- Any other language (#317).

#### Docs

`docs/product/sales.md` records:
- the job;
- where Share lives;
- the bill text and the lines that can be missing;
- the unit entry;
- the success criteria and metrics.

The glossary's **Sale bill** term carries its in-the-app wording: "Sale bill" is the selectable block's accessible name, and the action reads Share / Copy.

### Questions for the human

None. No finding stops a job from being completed.

### Observations on the design

- **Job 1, NuevaVenta → VentaDetalle:** in the prototype, "Guardá la venta" links to `Ventas.dc.html` rather than the sale's detail, so the main moment can't be walked there. `sales.md` wins; the drawing needs fixing outside this task.
- **VentaDetalle / VentaDetalleCompu:** neither drawing has the button, the toast, the failure notices, the selectable block or the loading state. The design is put together from existing components, so the design capture will be the first visual evidence.
- **Copied:** "Bill copied" may land while the "Sale saved" toast with Undo is still showing. The toast component doesn't say whether the two stack or one replaces the other. If one replaces the other, Undo goes away early. "Delete sale" still recovers, so this is friction.
- **Copy-failed notice, attention icon:** the PRD gives the icon to share-failed only. The rule says every failure notice gets it, and its closed list names neither line. This is unsettled.
- **Copy-failed block placement:** the block sits above the notice, so the notice's "the text below" points the wrong way. Placing the block right under the notice would fix it.
- **No bill:** the button disappears with no explanation, so the seller falls back to retyping. The PRD settled this; it is friction only.
- **The detail's other uses:** the flow leaves out "Assign customer", which `sales.md` puts on the detail. It competes for phone space with the button and "Delete sale".
- **Job 4, computer:** `VentasCompu` rows link to `VentasCompu` itself, so the list → detail step can't be walked in the prototype.
- **Loading:** the skeleton that stands in for the figures is not specified.
- **320 px with text at 130 %:** the scroll fallback must be checked in the design capture, the copy-failed state included.

## Tech Writer

_No `WRITING_STANDARDS.md`: the product has no writing criteria. These texts follow the request, the definition and the glossary only; the review proposes a starting document._

_Reaped 1 background process(es): 1 term_

### Texts

No `## Writing standards` section came with the task, so I wrote from the request, the PRD and the glossary alone. The only limit I could check is the 80-character line limit that the UI Designer's section cites, and both failure lines are within it. Catalog keys are proposed under the `saleBill` group in `web/src/copy/sales.ts`. The exact key names are the Tech Lead's call. The texts are not reviewed.

#### Sale's detail (`VentaDetalle`): the button

Place: the first item of the bottom action stack, above "Delete sale". It is never inside the "Sale saved" notice.

| Key | Text | When |
|---|---|---|
| `saleBill.share` | Share | The phone can share. Also its accessible name. |
| `saleBill.copy` | Copy | The phone can't share, or after a failed share. Also its accessible name. |

- The label is the bare word, with no "bill" or "sale". The icon is decorative, so the word alone is the accessible name.
- While loading, the button keeps the same word. It has no loading text of its own and shows no digits or `$`.
- When the server gives no bill, the button is absent and no text takes its place.

#### Sale's detail: toast after a successful copy

| Key | Text |
|---|---|
| `saleBill.copied` | Bill copied |

- It has no icon and no action.
- A successful share shows no text. A cancelled share shows no text.

#### Sale's detail: failure lines

| Key | Text | Attention icon |
|---|---|---|
| `saleBill.shareFailed` | Couldn't share. Tap Copy and paste it into the chat. | Yes, set beside the sentence. |
| `saleBill.copyFailed` | Couldn't copy. Select the text below and copy it by hand. | As the PRD says: none. |

- Each line says what failed and then what to do, in a short imperative. "Couldn't" is the contraction the PRD wrote.
- "Copy" in the share-failed line matches the label the button flips to.
- Open point for PM or UI Lead, not settled here: whether the copy-failed line also gets the attention icon. The PRD gives it to share-failed only. The text does not change either way.
- Placement note for the design: the copy-failed line says "the text below", so the selectable block must sit directly under this line.

#### Sale's detail: selectable block

| Key | Text |
|---|---|
| `saleBill.blockName` | Sale bill |

- This is the block's accessible name only. It is not a visible heading.
- The block's content is the bill text below, with its line breaks kept.

#### The bill text (what the customer reads)

This is plain text. It has no `*`, `_`, backtick or link, and no note, customer name, VIP, special or list wording. Fixed words come from the copy module. Business name, product names, dates and figures come from the sale.

| Key | Text |
|---|---|
| `saleBill.header` | `{business}` then a new line, then `{date}` (dd/mm, no year) |
| `saleBill.item` | `{product} {quantity} {unit} · {amount}` |
| `saleBill.bulk` | `{product} {quantity} {unit} · bulk price {unitPrice}/{unit} · {amount}` |
| `saleBill.gift` | `{product} {quantity} {unit} · Gift · $ 0` |
| `saleBill.shipping` | `Shipping · {amount}` |
| `saleBill.total` | `Total · {amount}` |
| `saleBill.paidCash` | Paid in cash |
| `saleBill.paidTransfer` | Paid by transfer |
| `saleBill.paid` | Paid |
| `saleBill.stillOwed` | `Still owed on this sale: {amount}` |
| `saleBill.balance` | `Your balance: {amount}` |
| `saleBill.credit` | `In your favor: {amount}` |
| `saleBill.unit` | g |

- The separator is " · ", a middle dot with a space on each side, as the request has it.
- Money is `$ 11.000`. Quantities are `0,01`, `0,1`, `1,05`, `3,5`, `1.000`.
- `saleBill.unit` is the one unit entry, "g" by default. Only this entry holds the "g". It is not translated word by word, and #318 makes it customizable.
- "Bulk price" and "Gift" use the glossary's in-the-app words. "Shipping" is the short form of "Shipping charged" and is kept short on purpose, because the customer reads it in a chat line.
- "Your balance" and "In your favor" use the glossary's "Owes you" and "In your favor" meaning, addressed to the customer. "In your favor" is the glossary's word for a credit.
- The gift's `$ 0` is fixed text in the money format, with no per-sale amount.

Order and layout:

```
{business}
{dd/mm}

{line}
{line}
Shipping · {amount}        (only when above $0)

Total · {amount}
{payment line}             (absent when the payment state is none)
{balance line}             (absent with no customer, or when the balance is exactly $0)
```

Example of how it must read, for the Tech Lead's hand-written cases. It is an illustration, not a generated expectation:

```
Flores Test
18/09

Aurora 3,5 g · $ 35.000
Luna 10 g · bulk price $ 9.000/g · $ 90.000
Mora 1 g · Gift · $ 0
Shipping · $ 2.000

Total · $ 127.000
Still owed on this sale: $ 27.000
Your balance: $ 41.000
```

#### Texts the task does not change

- **Glossary:** "Sale bill" is already the in-the-app word, the accessible name of the block. The action reads Share / Copy. No new term is needed.
- **Bot messages and API messages meant for a person:** none. The bill is built on the front, and an API refusal's `detail` is a diagnostic that is never shown.
- **No-bill message, "Sale saved" notice, Delete sale, not-found screen:** unchanged. No message is written for a hidden button, because the PRD settled that.
- **`en-XA`:** regenerated from these English texts, as the copy module requires. It is not written by hand.

## Interview: Tech Lead efímero→Tech Lead

### Round 1: questions

Round 1. I read `VentaDetalle.tsx`, `share.ts`, `clipboard.ts`, ADR-0053, `SaleBillOut`, `web/src/api/sales.ts` and the closed task documents.

### Where the current plan fails

**The bill goes stale or disappears after "Assign customer".**
- `onCustomerChosen` writes `{ ...updated, lines, total_grams }` into the cache, where `updated` is the `PUT` answer (`SaleOut`).
- That answer has no `bill`, and the front's `SaleDetail` type doesn't have the field yet.
- After assigning a customer, the cached detail therefore has no bill. The button disappears, or it renders an older bill that has no balance line.
- Showing the new customer's balance needs a fresh `GET`, because the settlement pass lives there (ADR-0053).
- The plan says "the text is always built from the last detail response", and this write breaks that.

### Questions

❓ **Q1 - Cache write after assign**: The assign path rebuilds the detail by hand from the `PUT` answer. Do you keep that and add `bill`, or refetch the detail?
- A hand-built `bill` would mean recomputing settlement in the front, which invariant 1 forbids.
- Without a bill the button disappears. With the old bill it shows a wrong text.

➡️ Recommendation: after a successful assign, invalidate and refetch `keys.sale(saleId)`. Don't carry `bill` through the spread. Add a Testing Library test: assign a customer and the button shows the new balance line.

❓ **Q2 - Where the front's `SaleBill` type comes from**: `web/src/api/sales.ts` has no `SaleBill`. The `satisfies` key lists and the OpenAPI-snapshot vitest hang on it.
- Which side wins when they disagree?
- Does `payment.state` use the server's `owed` and `paid`?
- `Sale['payment']` today uses `owing`, a different vocabulary on the same screen.

➡️ Recommendation: declare `SaleBill`, `SaleBillLine` and `SaleBillPayment` with literal unions copied from the snapshot's enums. The vitest compares both property names and enum values. Give the renderer an exhaustive `switch` over `state`, with no default branch, so a new server state fails `tsc --strict`.

❓ **Q3 - Synchronous click vs the share-failed flip**: `shareText` is async but the call itself is synchronous. After a failure the label flips to Copy.
- `canShare()` is read at render, and the flip is local state.
- The flip is lost on remount or refetch.
- On a failed share, a second tap runs `copyText` with the text rebuilt from fresh data, which is fine.
- What stops a double tap while a share is pending from opening two share sheets?

➡️ Recommendation: keep one `pending` ref, set synchronously in the handler, so a second tap does nothing while a share is open. Compute the label from `(canShare(), shareFailed)`. Test the `AbortError` path, the double tap, and the loading, failed and cancelled states.

❓ **Q4 - Where the toast and the failure lines live**: The "Sale saved" notice is cleared by a `navigate` with `replace`. "Bill copied" and the two failure lines are new local state on the same screen.
- Can "Bill copied" collide with the existing `AvisoExito` for "Customer assigned" (a single slot or two)?
- Does the copy-failed block, with its selectable text, need its own `WalkState` entry?
- Does the attention-icon failure line need one too?

➡️ Recommendation: reuse `AvisoExito` with a single `notice` state, so only one local message shows at a time. Add `WalkState` entries for copy-failed, share-failed and loading in `states*.ts`. Check the 44 px and `user-select` rules against the selectable block, which must not carry `user-select: none`.

❓ **Q5 - Splitting the front work**: One piece touches:
- `format/money.ts` plus `money.test.ts`
- `copy/sales.ts` plus the regenerated `en-XA`
- `sales/saleBill.ts` plus a 20-odd-case JSON file
- `api/sales.ts`
- `VentaDetalle.tsx` (already 382 lines)
- `states*.ts`
- the OpenAPI vitest
- the architecture guard
- `sales.md`

That is about six production modules, tests and docs. It is close to the "about four modules plus an ADR" cap, and the screen change needs its own seam.

➡️ Recommendation: cut it into two pieces.
- **Piece A** is pure: `formatQuantity`, the copy group, `en-XA`, the `SaleBill` types, `renderSaleBill`, the reference set, the guards and the snapshot vitest. It tests with no DOM.
- **Piece B** is the `SaleBillButton` component, the `VentaDetalle` wiring including Q1, `WalkState` and the docs.
- Piece B imports only A's exports.

### Invariants proposed
1. A write that updates the cached sale detail never carries `bill` over from a response that lacks it. Any such write invalidates the detail's query instead.
2. The renderer's `switch` over payment state and pricing rule is exhaustive, with no default branch.

### Estimate
You gave no figure this round, so give one. Closed tasks cost $103 to $242 each (GH-83 $105.55, GH-117 $103.32, GH-47 $123.23, GH-86 $169.42, GH-97 $242.31). I did not check how many subtasks each had. A single-piece plan with this scope would probably be split before it builds. The split in Q5 would give two pieces of about $100 each. The risk is that Piece B, with `VentaDetalle`, `WalkState` and the mobile e2e work, runs over.

### Round 1: answers

### Answers, round 1

I read `web/src/screens/ventas/VentaDetalle.tsx` (382 lines), `web/src/share.ts`, `web/src/clipboard.ts`, `web/src/api/sales.ts`, `web/src/api/keys.ts`, `app/sales/adapters/schemas.py` (`SaleBillOut`, `BillPaymentOut`, `SaleBillLineOut`), `app/sales/domain/sale_bill.py` (`BillState`, `BillRule`), `web/src/sales/grams.ts` and `web/src/mobile-standard/states*.ts`.

**Q1 – Cache write after assigning a customer.** Accepted. I'm also adding one thing the recommendation leaves open: the moment between the assign and the fresh detail arriving.
- **The hand write goes.** `onCustomerChosen` stops building the detail by hand: the `setQueryData(... { ...updated, lines: current.lines, total_grams: current.total_grams })` call is removed. Today's `invalidateResource(queryClient, 'sales')` already refetches `keys.sale(saleId)`, because that key starts with the same `'sales'` prefix (keys.ts:67). After the assign, `onCustomerChosen` awaits that refetch, with `queryClient.refetchQueries({ queryKey: keys.sale(saleId), exact: true })`, and only then sets "Customer assigned". The customer, the lines' costs and the bill then all come from one `GET`. That's the plan's own rule ("what is shown comes from the API's answer"), and it's the only way to get the new balance (ADR-0053, invariant 1).
- **The comment is rewritten.** The comment above that code, which says this saves a second `GET`, gets rewritten to give the new reason.
- **The type check catches it anyway.** Once `SaleDetail` has a required `bill: SaleBill | null`, a spread of `SaleOut` no longer type-checks. `tsc --strict` would flag the old code even if we forgot it.
- **The gap.** While the assign is pending or its refetch is in flight, the button keeps its label and is disabled with `aria-busy="true"`, like the loading state. A tap there can't share the old text with no balance line. A focus refetch does **not** disable the button, so it doesn't flicker on every return to the app. The text is built from whatever detail the cache holds at the tap.
- **Test** (Testing Library, `fetch` faked): a sale with no customer and a bill with `balance: null`. Assign a customer. The second `GET` answers with a balance. Copy, and the copied text ends in `Your balance: $ …`.
- I'm adopting your proposed invariant 1, worded as: *no write puts a sale detail into the cache by hand; a write that changes the sale invalidates `keys.sale(id)` and the screen reads the refetch.*

**Q2 – Where the front's `SaleBill` type comes from.** Accepted. Here is what I'm adding.
- **The types.** `web/src/api/sales.ts` declares `SaleBill`, `SaleBillLine` and `SaleBillPayment`, with `BillState = 'cash' | 'transfer' | 'paid' | 'owed' | 'none'` and `BillRule = 'bulk' | 'gift'`, copied from `app/sales/domain/sale_bill.py`. `SaleDetail` gains `bill: SaleBill | null`.
- **The server wins.** The OpenAPI snapshot is what the server answers. The vitest fails when the front's key lists or enum lists differ from it, and the fix is always on the front.
- **Two vocabularies, on purpose.** `Sale['payment']` keeps `'owing'` and the bill keeps `'owed'`. They are different facts: the sale's payment method versus the bill's settled state. Merging them would change an existing type with no change in behaviour, so I'm not doing it.
- **The key lists.** The vitest compares property names for `SaleBillOut`, `SaleBillLineOut` and `BillPaymentOut`, plus the `state` and `rule` enums. The front's lists are declared `as const satisfies readonly (keyof SaleBill)[]`, and the same for the others.
- **Quantity.** `quantity` is `GramsOut`, the same shape as every other grams field. The renderer passes it through `hundredthsOfApiGrams` (`web/src/sales/grams.ts`), then `formatQuantity`, and never does float arithmetic.
- **Exhaustive switches.** The renderer has an exhaustive `switch` over `state` and over `rule`, with no `default` branch, closed by a `never` assignment. A new server value then fails `tsc`, and the snapshot vitest fails too. I'm adopting your proposed invariant 2 as written.

**Q3 – A double tap while a share is open, and the share-failed flip.** Accepted.
- **Pending guard.** A `useRef<boolean>` named `pending` is checked and set as the handler's first statement. A tap while it is set does nothing. It is cleared when the `shareText` or `copyText` promise settles, in a `finally`. Copy uses the same guard: on a secure context `copyText` is async too.
- **Synchronous call.** Before the call the handler only renders the text and sets the ref, with no `await`. The `execCommand` fallback stays inside the gesture.
- **Label.** The label is worked out on each render from `canShare()` and a local `shareFailed` state. Losing `shareFailed` on remount is fine: it means a new visit, and Share is offered again.
- **Tests:** Share calls `shareText` with the text; `AbortError` shows nothing and the label stays Share; a non-abort failure shows the failure line and the label becomes Copy; a double tap with the share promise held calls `navigator.share` once; with `isSecureContext=false`, `execCommand('copy')` is called synchronously in the click and the toast shows; copy failure; `bill: null`; loading.

**Q4 – Where the toast and the failure lines live.** Mostly accepted. I'm narrowing the `WalkState` part.
- **One toast slot.** "Customer assigned" and "Bill copied" share one `notice` state (`'assigned' | 'copied' | null`) shown through the existing `AvisoExito`, with the existing timer. They can't both show. The newer one replaces the older.
- **The failure lines are not toasts.** They are separate local state that stays until the next tap. Share failed is a line with the attention icon. Copy failed is the line plus the bill block. Starting a new tap clears both, and a successful copy clears them as well.
- **The bill block** is a `div` with `role="region"` and `aria-label` = "Sale bill" (from copy), wrapping a `<pre>` with `white-space: pre-wrap` and an explicit `user-select: text`. It's not a `textarea`, so the input rules don't apply to it. It's not a button, so `user-select: none` and the 44 px target don't either. The contrast and 12 px text-size checks still apply, tokens only.
- **WalkState.** `copy-failed` and `loading` are added in `statesSales.ts`, as the PRD requires.
  - The loading state holds `GET /sales/{id}`, then checks the disabled, busy button with no digit or `$`.
  - Copy-failed is reached with copy forced to fail in the walk's page.
  - Share-failed is **not** a walk state. It's a one-line notice with the icon, not a form, sheet or confirmation. Walking it would mean faking `navigator.share` in the e2e context, which the walk doesn't do today. Testing Library covers it.
- **Placement.** The button goes in `venta-detalle__acciones` next to "Assign customer" and the delete icon, as a `secondary` `Boton`. It is never inside the "Sale saved" notice. The design capture is checked before delivery.

**Q5 – Splitting the front work.** Accepted: two pieces, built in order.
- **Piece A (pure, no DOM):**
  - `formatQuantity` in `format/money.ts`, with `formatGrams` delegating to it; its existing tests stay unchanged and new cases are added;
  - the `saleBill` copy group and the regenerated `en-XA`;
  - the `SaleBill*` types in `api/sales.ts`;
  - `sales/saleBill.ts` (`renderSaleBill`);
  - the hand-written `saleBill.cases.json`, including PM's $0-balance case and every case under Success criteria;
  - the unit-override test, the leak test, the guard against a "g" literal or `formatGrams`, and the OpenAPI-snapshot vitest.
- **Piece B (screen):**
  - a `SaleBillButton` component holding the label, the pending ref, the outcomes and the bill block;
  - the `VentaDetalle` wiring: the Q1 cache change, the busy state, and the shared toast slot;
  - the Testing Library tests;
  - the `WalkState` entries and a green mobile e2e run with no new offender;
  - `docs/product/sales.md` checked against what was built.
- B imports only A's exports. Taking the button out of `VentaDetalle` also keeps that 382-line file from growing much.
- **Old shapes stay.** `Sale['payment']`'s `'owing'` and the existing Spanish-named components (`AvisoExito`, `Boton`, `VentaDetalle`) stay as they are. New modules use the glossary's English names.

### Invariants (adding to the request's seven)
8. No write puts a sale detail into the cache by hand. A write that changes a sale invalidates `keys.sale(id)`, and the bill is always the one from the last `GET`. While an assign is pending or its refetch is in flight, the button is disabled and busy.
9. The renderer's `switch` over payment state and pricing rule is exhaustive, with no default branch.
10. Share and Copy are each guarded by one synchronous pending flag: a tap while a share or copy is unresolved does nothing.

### Estimate
- **Piece A:** about $90. It's pure code and tests; most of the cost is writing the ~25 reference cases by hand and regenerating `en-XA`.
- **Piece B:** about $130. Most of the cost is the walk and mobile e2e work, plus the capture.
- **Total:** about $220, at the upper end of the closed tasks ($103 to $242). The risk is in B: if the copy-failed walk state needs new support in the walk to make copy fail, B could reach about $170.

### Round 2: questions

Round 2. I checked `keys.ts` and your claim holds: `sale(id)` starts with `PREFIX.sales`, so `invalidateResource('sales')` already refetches the detail. Q1 to Q5 are settled. Below are what the answers leave open, found in the code.

### Where the amended plan still fails

**A `bill` field required in Piece A does not type-check on its own.**
- Piece A makes `SaleDetail.bill` required. The hand-written cache spread in `VentaDetalle.tsx`, which Piece B removes, then fails `tsc --strict`.
- So do the `SaleDetail` fixtures. `total_grams` appears in `VentaDetalle.test.tsx`, `NuevaVenta.test.tsx` and `backCards.test.tsx`, besides `grams.test.ts`.
- Piece A cannot merge green by itself.

### Questions

❓ **Q1 - Who owns the type's blast radius**: Piece A adds `bill: SaleBill | null`, which breaks the spread in `VentaDetalle.tsx` and every `SaleDetail` fixture, all in B's files.
- Which piece makes `tsc`, `ruff` and `mypy` green?
- What does Piece A leave in `VentaDetalle.tsx`?

➡️ Recommendation: Piece A also owns the whole removal of the hand write: the `setQueryData` spread, the awaited refetch and its test. It also adds `bill: null` to the fixtures. It then merges green, and B is purely additive: the button, the busy state and the walk states. The removal is about 15 lines and already needs its own seam.

❓ **Q2 - The loading state has no button**: `VentaDetalleScreen` returns `<Loading/>` alone while `isPending`, with no layout. The PRD wants the button with its label, disabled and busy, with no digit or `$`, while loading. The mobile standard wants the content area "shaped like its final layout" with `aria-busy`.
- Where does a disabled button come from when the early return renders none?
- A pending query has no bill to render, so the button must show without one.

➡️ Recommendation: in the pending branch, render the same `.cuerpo` with the title row skeleton, `Loading` and a disabled `SaleBillButton` (`busy`, label from `canShare()`, no data props). The `loading` WalkState then asserts it. Check that the `chrome-shift` and `content-shift` budgets hold when the real layout arrives.

❓ **Q3 - A failed awaited refetch**: After the assign succeeds, you now await the refetch before "Customer assigned".
- If that `GET` fails (offline, 5xx), the `isError` branch runs first and replaces the whole screen with `SinConexion` or `ErrorGenerico`. The assignment already happened and the toast never shows.
- If the refetch is a 404 (a partner deleted the sale), a `GoneScreen` appears with no "gone" navigation.
- The same replacement already happens on a focus refetch.

➡️ Recommendation: decide it explicitly. After a successful `PUT`, show "Customer assigned" first. Then refetch with `refetchQueries` (it does not throw). If that fails, keep the last detail on screen and clear the busy state, with no error screen. Cover it with a test: the assign succeeds, the second `GET` fails, and the screen still shows the sale.

❓ **Q4 - `*`, `_` and backtick can still reach the text**: The PRD's leak test asserts the whole text has none of them. But product names and the business name are the seller's own words, and flower names such as "Blue_Dream" or "OG*Kush" are plausible. WhatsApp would read `_x_` as italics.
- The renderer only controls its fixed words, so the claim "the text has none" is false for seller data.
- Sanitising would silently change a name the customer sees. Not sanitising makes the stated criterion untrue.

➡️ Recommendation: the leak test checks the fixed words and fixture names without those characters. Ask PM whether criterion 3 means the fixed words only, and record the answer in `sales.md`. Put a reference-set case with a name containing `_` and show the text unchanged. Don't sanitise.

❓ **Q5 - Pending flag and a share that never settles**: Invariant 10 clears the flag in a `finally` on the promise. Some browsers leave `navigator.share` unresolved when the user dismisses the sheet. The button would then stay dead until the screen remounts.
- Share has no timeout, and the label stays Share.

➡️ Recommendation: keep the flag, but also clear it on `visibilitychange` back to visible (the page returns from the sheet). Test: the share promise is held, a `visibilitychange` event fires, and the next tap calls share. If you reject this, say that a hung share is a known risk.

### Invariants proposed
1. A `SaleDetail` in a test fixture or a mock always carries `bill`, as `null` or a whole bill, so no test builds a detail the API could not answer.
2. The sale bill's reference cases include seller-typed names with `_` and `*` unchanged, and no renderer code rewrites a product or business name.

### Estimate
You gave $90 for Piece A and $130 for Piece B. If Piece A takes the removal and the fixtures (Q1), A is about $100 and B about $110. The closed tasks ($103 to $242) were whole tasks, so a $220 two-piece total is at the high end. The walk-state work in B has no precedent in the tasks I read. I would plan for B near $170, as you said.

### Round 2: answers

### Answers, round 2

I read `VentaDetalle.tsx`'s error and pending branches (lines 245–276) and the sale bill section of `docs/product/sales.md` (lines 148–149 and 320–336).

**Q1 – Who fixes what the new `bill` field breaks.** Accepted. Piece A merges green on its own.
- **What A owns.** Piece A adds `bill: SaleBill | null` to `SaleDetail`. In the same diff it also does three things:
  - removes the `setQueryData` spread in `onCustomerChosen`;
  - adds the awaited refetch, in the shape Q3 below decides;
  - adds `bill: null` to every `SaleDetail` fixture and mock (`VentaDetalle.test.tsx`, `VentaDetalle.sheets.test.tsx`, `NuevaVenta.test.tsx`, `backCards.test.tsx` and any other place `tsc` finds).
- **Its test.** A adds the assign test, made to fit a piece with no button: assign a customer, the second `GET` answers, and the screen shows the new customer from that `GET`. Piece B adds the assertion that a copy after an assign carries the balance line.
- **Checks.** There's no backend change, so `ruff` and `mypy` are untouched. `tsc` and `oxlint` go green in A.
- **Piece B is purely additive:** the `SaleBillButton`, the busy state, the shared toast slot, the walk states and the docs check.
- **Invariant 1 proposed:** adopted as written.
- **Cost shift.** This moves about $10 from B to A.

**Q2 – A loading state with no button.** Accepted.
- **The pending branch.** It stops returning a bare `<Loading/>`. It renders the same `.cuerpo` with `aria-busy="true"`, holding:
  - an empty title row: the real title has a date, and digits are forbidden while loading;
  - `Loading`;
  - the `venta-detalle__acciones` row with a disabled `SaleBillButton busy` and no bill. Its label comes from `canShare()`.
- **The props.** `SaleBillButton` takes `bill: SaleBill | null | undefined`, where `undefined` means "still loading". It shows the busy, disabled button whenever `bill` is `undefined` or the `busy` prop is set. When `bill` is `null` it renders nothing.
- **Mobile checks.** The `loading` WalkState holds `GET /sales/{id}`. It checks the label is present, the button is `disabled` and `aria-busy`, and there's no digit or `$`.
- **Shift budgets.** `chrome-shift` must stay at 0, and `content-shift` within `CONTENT_SHIFT_PX`. If putting the action row at the bottom of the skeleton breaks `content-shift`, the skeleton's row moves to where it sits in the final layout. That's a layout fix, not a new offender.
- **Coexistence.** The other pending branches in the app keep their bare `Loading`. Only this screen changes.

**Q3 – The awaited refetch fails.** Accepted, with a correction. `refetchQueries` doesn't throw, but in TanStack Query v5 a failed refetch still puts the query in `isError`, with `data` kept. Today's `if (isError)` would still replace the screen, so the order of the branches must change:
- **The order.**
  1. After a successful `PUT`, `onCustomerChosen` sets the "Customer assigned" notice first.
  2. It then awaits `refetchQueries({ queryKey: keys.sale(saleId), exact: true })` with the button busy.
  3. It clears the busy state whatever the outcome.
- **The screen's branches.** The full-screen error branches (`SinConexion`, `ErrorGenerico`) run only when `isError && data === undefined`. When `data` is present and a refetch fails with a network or server error, the last detail stays on screen and the button is enabled again. The text is built from the last detail the screen holds, which is what the PRD says. This applies to a focus refetch too, and that's an intended fix: a passing network blip no longer wipes a loaded sale.
- **A 404 with data present** (a partner deleted the sale) is not silent. It takes the existing gone path, as `removeSale` does: `invalidateResource(queryClient, 'sales', { refetchType: 'none' })`, then `navigate(SALES, { replace: true, state: gone() })`. An effect on `error` handles it. A 404 with no data keeps today's `GoneScreen`.
- **Tests (in A):**
  - assign succeeds, the second `GET` fails offline → the sale and "Customer assigned" still show, with no error screen;
  - assign succeeds, the second `GET` answers 404 → lands on Sales with the gone notice.

**Q4 – `*`, `_` and backtick in names the seller typed.** Accepted: no sanitising, and the question goes to PM.
- **The conflict.** `sales.md:329` currently says the bill never holds "Markdown markers (`*`, `_`, backtick)". Taken literally, that covers the business name and product names, which are the seller's own data. Rewriting them would show the customer a name that differs from the seller's.
- **What I build until PM answers:**
  - the leak test checks the renderer's fixed words, and runs over fixtures whose names have none of these characters;
  - one reference case, "Blue_Dream" with a business name containing `*`, expects the text unchanged, written by hand;
  - no renderer code touches a product or business name.
- **Invariant 2 proposed:** adopted. If PM's answer is "the whole text", that's a behaviour change (escaping or stripping) and comes back as a new decision.

**Q5 – A share promise that never settles.** Accepted.
- **When the flag clears.** The pending flag clears in the promise's `finally`, and also when a `visibilitychange` event brings the document back to `visible`. The listener is added at the tap and removed when the flag clears or the component unmounts. Copy uses the same flag. Its fallback is synchronous and its async path settles, so the listener changes nothing for Copy.
- **The leftover promise.** If the old promise settles after a new tap, it must not clear the new tap's flag or show its outcome. Each tap gets a sequence number in a ref, and only the latest one may clear the flag or set state.
- **Test:** hold the share promise, fire `visibilitychange` back to visible, tap again → `navigator.share` is called a second time. Then resolve the first promise as failed → no failure line shows.
- **Known risk.** A browser that neither settles the share nor fires `visibilitychange` leaves the button dead until the screen remounts. I accept that risk.

### Invariants (adding to round 1's)
11. Every `SaleDetail` in a fixture or mock carries `bill`, as `null` or a whole bill.
12. The reference set holds names the seller typed with `_` and `*`, unchanged, and no renderer code rewrites a product or business name (unless PM rules otherwise).
13. A failed refetch never replaces a sale detail already on screen, except a 404, which takes the gone path.
14. Only the latest tap's outcome may clear the pending flag or change what the screen shows.

### Estimate
- **Piece A:** about $100. It now includes the removal of the hand write, the error-branch reordering, the fixtures and their tests.
- **Piece B:** I plan around $150, with $170 as the ceiling because of the walk states. Most of B's cost is getting the walk to make copy fail and the skeleton's shift budgets.
- **Total:** about $250–270, above the highest closed task ($242).

### Round 2: PM answers

### Answers to the Tech Lead (round 2)

**Q1: `*`, `_` and backtick in names the seller typed.** Your default is correct. The rule covers only the bill's own fixed words. Names the seller typed go out exactly as typed, even when they contain these characters.

- **The bill never changes the seller's data.** The business name and every variety name are the seller's own data. The bill sends them exactly as stored: nothing escaped, removed or replaced. The customer reads the same name the seller sees in the app, which matches criterion 21: the customer sees the same things I do.
- **The no-`*`, `_` or backtick rule is about the bill's own words.** The bill adds no formatting of its own. No fixed word, separator or format in the copy module holds these characters. That is what keeps the bill plain text.
- **Escaping would break Assume nothing.** Which characters count as formatting depends on the chat app: Telegram, WhatsApp and others each have different rules, and some have none. Escaping for one app would build a channel into the bill, and the job deliberately names "the chat", never a channel. How a chat app shows a name is that app's business.
- **What this means for the tests:**
  - The leak test checks the fixed text. Its fixture names hold none of these characters, so any `*`, `_` or backtick it finds came from the bill itself.
  - The reference set gets one more case: a variety named with `_`, `*` and a backtick (e.g. ``OG*Kush_`x` ``), whose line shows the name unchanged.

I clarified `docs/product/sales.md` to match: the "Never in the bill" line, criterion 22, the Sale bill facts, the leak-test metric and the reference set.

### Round 3: questions

Round 3. I checked `keys.ts`: a payment write invalidates `sales` (`CROSS_INVALIDATES.payments`), so the balance on this device is not stale. A partner's payment on another device is covered by the refetch on focus. Q1 to Q5 of round 2 are settled. What follows is what the amended plan leaves open.

### Where the amended plan still fails

**The error-branch change is a behaviour change outside the PRD.**
- You now show the last detail when a refetch fails: `isError && data === undefined`, where today any `isError` replaces the screen.
- You also add a 404 effect that navigates away.
- The PRD says "A 404 takes the existing not-found path" and "the detail keeps its existing invalidations and refetches on focus". It says nothing about a blip no longer wiping a loaded sale.
- `VentaDetalle.test.tsx` may already assert the old behaviour. Every other detail screen keeps the old one, so this screen would be the odd one out.

### Questions

❓ **Q1 - Piece A has grown two unrelated jobs**: A now holds:
- the pure side: `formatQuantity`, the copy group, `en-XA`, `renderSaleBill`, the reference cases and guards;
- the screen's data flow: `SaleBill` types, `bill` on `SaleDetail`, the fixtures, the awaited refetch, the error-branch reordering, the 404 effect and their tests.

Counting production modules, that is `money.ts`, `copy/sales.ts` and the locale, `saleBill.ts`, `api/sales.ts` and `VentaDetalle.tsx`, plus a large hand-written JSON. The two halves share no seam except the types.

➡️ Recommendation: three pieces.
- **A0** is the data flow: types, `bill` on `SaleDetail`, fixtures, assign refetch, error branches and their tests. It has no renderer.
- **A** is the pure renderer.
- **B** is the button, as before.
- A imports A0's types, so A0 goes first. B imports A's exports.
- Each piece has its own seam and stays well under the cap. If you keep two pieces, say which of the modules above you would drop from A.

❓ **Q2 - The error-branch rule needs PM**: Q3's change is not in the PRD.
- Either the PRD takes it as written and the screen keeps its old branches, or PM approves a keep-the-data rule for this screen.
- A conflict between those two is a PM call. I do not decide behaviour.
- Without PM, the safe default is the old behaviour. After a successful assign, a failed refetch then shows the existing error screen. The assignment is already done, so on a retry the detail loads with the customer.

➡️ Recommendation: ask PM now (it comes back with your next answers). Until then, keep the old branches, and move the keep-the-data rule and the 404 effect to a later task. Tell PM the cost of leaving it: after an assign, a flaky network can show an error screen over a sale that was saved. If PM approves, add a test that pins the old behaviour of the other detail screens, so the rule stays local to this one.

❓ **Q3 - `undefined` as "loading"**: `SaleBillButton` takes `bill: SaleBill | null | undefined`, with `undefined` meaning loading and `null` meaning no bill.
- A caller that forgets to pass `bill` would show a permanent disabled busy button instead of a type error.
- With `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` off, an omitted prop is also `undefined`.

➡️ Recommendation: make the state explicit: a prop `state: { kind: 'loading' } | { kind: 'ready'; bill: SaleBill | null }`, and a separate `refreshing` flag for the assign refetch. The compiler then forces every caller to say which one it means.

### Invariants proposed
1. The sale bill's button takes its loading state as an explicit value, never as a missing or `undefined` bill.

Each piece is then below the lowest closed whole task ($103) except B. I have not verified the subtask counts of those tasks.

**Q3 – The button's loading state is explicit.** Accepted.
- **The props.** `SaleBillButton` takes `state: { kind: 'loading' } | { kind: 'ready'; bill: SaleBill | null }`, required, plus a required `refreshing: boolean`.
- **What each value shows:**
  - `loading`, or `ready` with `refreshing`, gives the busy, disabled button, with no digit or `$`;
  - `ready` with `bill: null` renders nothing;
  - `ready` with a bill gives the working button.
- **The callers.** The pending branch passes `{ kind: 'loading' }` and `refreshing={false}`. The loaded branch passes `{ kind: 'ready', bill: sale.bill }` and the flag from Q2. Neither prop is optional, so a forgotten one is a type error.

### Closing

The interview is closed: the frontier is empty, and what remains is a known risk.

### Decided
- **Three pieces, built in order.**
  - **A0** is the data flow:
    - the `SaleBill` types and `bill` on `SaleDetail`;
    - `bill: null` in every `SaleDetail` fixture and mock;
    - the snapshot vitest;
    - removing the hand-written cache write after an assign, so the detail is refetched.
  - **A** is the pure side: `formatQuantity`, the copy group, `en-XA`, `renderSaleBill`, the hand-written reference set and the guards.
  - **B** is the screen: the `SaleBillButton`, the shared notice slot, the pending skeleton, the `copy-failed` and `loading` walk states and the docs check.
- **The detail's error branches stay as today.** The keep-the-data rule and the 404 effect are withdrawn, so no PM consult is needed.
- **The bill's text.** PM ruled that the seller's own names go out unchanged. The no-`*`, `_` or backtick rule covers only the fixed words.
- **The button's state.** The loading state is an explicit required prop, never an `undefined` bill. The renderer's switches are exhaustive. Share and Copy share one pending flag guarded by a sequence number, which a `visibilitychange` back to visible also clears.
- **Estimate:** about $270 in total (A0 $55, A $85, B $130 up to $170). That is above the highest closed task (GH-97, $242), which is why the work is cut into three pieces.

### One concrete way it can still fail
The busy state after an assign clears when `dataUpdatedAt` moves.
- A focus refetch that started before the `PUT` can land after it. It carries the pre-assign detail, which has no customer and no balance line.
- That clears the flag early, and a tap then builds a text without the balance.
- Fix, which I recommend you adopt in B: clear the flag only when the loaded detail shows a customer (`sale.customer !== null`), or when the query errors. Do not clear it on a timestamp.
- Add a test: hold a stale refetch, finish the assign, release the stale response, and the button stays busy until the detail with the customer arrives.

### Known risks
- **The walk states.** `copy-failed` may need walk support to make copy fail, so B could reach $170.
- **The skeleton's shift budgets.** The loading skeleton's `content-shift` budget is unproven until the e2e runs.
- **A hung share.** A browser that neither settles `navigator.share` nor fires `visibilitychange` leaves the button dead until the screen remounts.
- **A failed refetch after an assign.** It shows today's error screen over a sale that was saved. That is today's behaviour for any refetch, and a retry loads the sale with its customer.
- **Hand-written reference set.** The reference set only proves what its hand-written cases cover.

## Tech Lead

_Reaped 1 background process(es): 1 term_

### Approach

This is part 2 of GH-322 and is front-only. The backend already answers `bill` on `GET /sales/{id}` (#375: `SaleBillOut`, `SaleBillLineOut`, `BillPaymentOut`, with `BillState` and `BillRule` in `app/sales/domain/sale_bill.py`). ADR-0053 says a shareable text is built in the front from facts the server sends. Nothing in `app/` changes.

The work is three subtasks, built in this order. Each one merges green on its own.

1. **`sale-detail-bill-data`**: the data flow.
   - The `SaleBill` types in `web/src/api/sales.ts`, and `bill: SaleBill | null` on `SaleDetail`.
   - The OpenAPI-snapshot key-list vitest.
   - `bill` added to every `SaleDetail` fixture.
   - The hand-written cache write after "Assign customer" goes. It rebuilt the detail from the `PUT`'s `SaleOut`, which has no `bill`, so it would drop the bill or leave a stale one. The detail is refetched instead, through the existing `invalidateResource(queryClient, 'sales')`, because `keys.sale(id)` shares the `sales` prefix.
2. **`sale-bill-renderer`**: the pure side, with no DOM.
   - `formatQuantity` in `web/src/format/money.ts`.
   - The `saleBill` copy group, with `en-XA` regenerated.
   - `renderSaleBill` in `web/src/sales/saleBill.ts`.
   - The hand-written `saleBill.cases.json`, plus the leak test, the unit test and the guard.
3. **`sale-bill-button`**: the screen.
   - A new `SaleBillButton` component, wired into `VentaDetalle`.
   - One notice slot shared with "Customer assigned".
   - A loading skeleton that already holds the busy button.
   - The `copy-failed` and `loading` walk states, and the docs checked against what was built.

### Decisions that hold across subtasks

- **Settlement is never recomputed on the front.** The bill shown is always the one from the last `GET /sales/{id}`. No write puts a sale detail into the cache by hand.
- **The detail's error branches stay as they are today.** That covers `SinConexion`, `ErrorGenerico` and `GoneScreen` on 404. A failed refetch after an assign shows today's error screen. The assignment is already saved, so a retry loads the sale with its customer.
- **Two payment vocabularies stay apart on purpose.** `Sale['payment']` keeps `'owing'`. The bill's `BillState` uses the server's `'cash' | 'transfer' | 'paid' | 'owed' | 'none'`. They are different facts: how the sale was paid, versus where settlement leaves it.
- **The OpenAPI snapshot wins.** When the front's key lists disagree with `tests/fixtures/openapi_snapshot.json`, the fix is on the front.
- **Seller-typed names go out exactly as stored** (PM, round 2). The business name and product names are never escaped or stripped. The rule against `*`, `_` and backtick covers only the bill's fixed words, separators and formats.
- **The button's label** is worked out on each render from `canShare()` and a local `shareFailed` state. Losing `shareFailed` on remount is intended.
- **Double taps.** One pending flag, set synchronously, guards Share and Copy together, and a sequence number decides which tap's outcome counts. The flag also clears when `visibilitychange` brings the page back to visible, which covers browsers that never settle a dismissed share.
- **After an assign, the button stays busy until the fresh detail arrives.** It clears when the loaded detail shows a customer (`sale.customer !== null`) or when the query errors. It never clears on a timestamp, because a focus refetch that started before the `PUT` could carry the detail from before the assign.
- **Both failure lines carry the attention icon.** The copy-failed line says something failed, so the glossary's Attention icon rule covers it as well as the share-failed line.
- **Old shapes stay.** Existing Spanish-named components (`VentaDetalle`, `AvisoExito`, `Boton`, `SinConexion`) and CSS classes (`venta-detalle__*`) are not renamed. New modules use the glossary's English names (`SaleBill`, `renderSaleBill`, `SaleBillButton`, `formatQuantity`).

### Security and privacy (§9, §10)

- No new route or input, and nothing widens what a request reaches.
- The bill holds no note, customer name, ids, VIP, special or list wording, or links. `SaleBillOut` is closed on the server.
- The text is never logged, put in `console`, or stored in `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`. It appears on screen only when copying fails.
- `share.ts` and `clipboard.ts` stay the only modules that name `navigator.share`, `navigator.clipboard` or `execCommand`.

### Performance

The cost doesn't grow with data: rendering is linear in the lines of one sale, and there is no new query.

### Known risks

- **The `copy-failed` walk state** may need new support in the walk to make copy fail. That is the main cost risk in the third subtask.
- **The loading skeleton's `content-shift` budget** is unproven until the mobile e2e runs.
- **A hung share.** A browser that neither settles `navigator.share` nor fires `visibilitychange` leaves the button dead until the screen remounts. This risk is accepted.
- **The reference set** only proves what its hand-written cases cover.

### Estimate

About $270 in total: about $55 for the data flow, $85 for the renderer, and $130 to $170 for the button. This is a part of a split, so it is built as three subtasks and not split again.

### Subtasks

`### Subtasks` written by the system from the structured plan: no exact `### Subtasks` line (outside code fences) was found in this section, so the block below was built from the structured plan. Any list the Tech Lead wrote under another heading is not the one the Developers read.

#### Carry the sale bill on the front's sale detail and refetch it after assigning a customer

Front only (`web/`). `GET /sales/{id}` already answers `bill` (see `SaleBillOut`, `SaleBillLineOut`, `BillPaymentOut` in `app/sales/adapters/schemas.py`, and `BillState`/`BillRule` in `app/sales/domain/sale_bill.py`). This subtask makes the front carry it.

1. **Types** in `web/src/api/sales.ts`:
   - `BillState = 'cash' | 'transfer' | 'paid' | 'owed' | 'none'`;
   - `BillRule = 'bulk' | 'gift'`;
   - `SaleBillPayment { state: BillState; still_owed: number | null }`;
   - `SaleBillLine { product: string; quantity: GramsOut; amount: number; rule: BillRule | null; unit_price: number | null }`;
   - `SaleBill { business_name; date; shipping_charged; total; lines: SaleBillLine[]; payment: SaleBillPayment; balance: number | null }`, with `date` typed like `SaleDetail.date`.
   - Add the required field `bill: SaleBill | null` to `SaleDetail`.
   - Keep `Sale['payment']` with `'owing'` as it is. The two vocabularies stay apart on purpose.
   - Export the key lists:
     - `SALE_BILL_KEYS`, `SALE_BILL_LINE_KEYS`, `SALE_BILL_PAYMENT_KEYS`, each declared `as const satisfies readonly (keyof X)[]`;
     - `BILL_STATES` and `BILL_RULES`, declared `as const satisfies readonly BillState[]` and `readonly BillRule[]`.
2. **Contract vitest.** It reads `tests/fixtures/openapi_snapshot.json`. For the `SaleBillOut`, `SaleBillLineOut` and `BillPaymentOut` schemas, it asserts that the property-name sets equal the key lists, and that the `state` and `rule` enum sets equal `BILL_STATES` and `BILL_RULES`. It asserts `bill` is a property of `SaleDetailOut`. The snapshot is the truth: on a mismatch, the front is what changes.
3. **Fixtures.** Every `SaleDetail` in a test fixture or mock gets `bill`, as `null` or a whole bill. That covers `web/src/screens/ventas/VentaDetalle.test.tsx`, `VentaDetalle.sheets.test.tsx`, `NuevaVenta.test.tsx`, `web/src/architecture/backCards.test.tsx`, and wherever else `tsc` points.
4. **Assign refetch.** In `web/src/screens/ventas/VentaDetalle.tsx`, `onCustomerChosen`:
   - Remove the `queryClient.setQueryData<SaleDetail>(keys.sale(saleId), ...)` spread of the `PUT` answer.
   - Keep `invalidateResource(queryClient, 'sales')`. It already refetches `keys.sale(saleId)`, because the key shares the `sales` prefix.
   - Keep `setAssigned(true)` straight after the `PUT` succeeds.
   - Rewrite the comment above it: the detail is now read back from `GET`, because the bill's balance comes from the settlement pass only that read does (ADR-0053).
   - Change nothing else. The `isError`, offline, generic-error and `GoneScreen` branches stay exactly as today, and so do the assign error paths.

**Acceptance:**
- `tsc` strict, `oxlint` and vitest pass, with nothing silenced.
- Testing Library, `fetch` faked: a sale with no customer and `bill.balance: null`. Assign a customer; the second `GET /sales/{id}` answers with the customer. The screen shows the customer's name from that second response, and two `GET`s were made.
- The existing `VentaDetalle` tests pass unchanged, apart from adding `bill` to their fixtures.

Seams: `GET /sales/{id} faked at fetch, through the VentaDetalle screen with Testing Library`, `tests/fixtures/openapi_snapshot.json against the exported key lists of web/src/api/sales.ts`.

Depends on: nothing.

#### Format quantities from hundredths and render the sale bill's plain text from copy

Pure TypeScript with no DOM, in `web/`. It imports the `SaleBill` types from `web/src/api/sales.ts`.

1. **`formatQuantity(hundredths: number): string`** in `web/src/format/money.ts`.
   - Integer arithmetic only: the whole part `Math.trunc(h / 100)` with dot thousands, then, when the remainder is non-zero, a comma and two digits with the trailing zero dropped.
   - Cases: 1 → "0,01", 10 → "0,1", 105 → "1,05", 350 → "3,5", 100000 → "1.000", 0 → "0".
   - `formatGrams(grams)` becomes `formatQuantity(Math.round(Number(grams) * 100)) + ' g'`, with today's exact output. Its existing tests stay untouched; add the `formatQuantity` cases to `money.test.ts`.
2. **Copy** in `web/src/copy/sales.ts`: a `saleBill` group, following the module's existing patterns and ADR-0049/0050. Texts (from the Tech Writer):
   - `share` "Share"; `copy` "Copy"; `copied` "Bill copied";
   - `shareFailed` "Couldn't share. Tap Copy and paste it into the chat.";
   - `copyFailed` "Couldn't copy. Select the text below and copy it by hand.";
   - `blockName` "Sale bill";
   - `header(business, date)` → business, `\n`, date;
   - `item(product, quantity, unit, amount)` → `{product} {quantity} {unit} · {amount}`;
   - `bulk(product, quantity, unit, unitPrice, amount)` → `{product} {quantity} {unit} · bulk price {unitPrice}/{unit} · {amount}`;
   - `gift(product, quantity, unit)` → `{product} {quantity} {unit} · Gift · $ 0`;
   - `shipping(amount)` → `Shipping · {amount}`; `total(amount)` → `Total · {amount}`;
   - `paidCash` "Paid in cash"; `paidTransfer` "Paid by transfer"; `paid` "Paid";
   - `stillOwed(amount)` → `Still owed on this sale: {amount}`;
   - `balance(amount)` → `Your balance: {amount}`; `credit(amount)` → `In your favor: {amount}`;
   - `unit` "g".
   The separator is " · ". Regenerate `en-XA`, and update the baseline files the copy module's staleness and limit tests require, with its scripts, never by hand. Register the strings in `LIMITED_COPY` where the module's limits require it.
3. **`renderSaleBill(bill: SaleBill, text = SALE_BILL_TEXT): string`** in `web/src/sales/saleBill.ts`. Pure; the copy group is injectable so a test can override the unit. Output joined by `\n`:
   - the header, then an empty line;
   - one line per bill line: `rule === 'bulk'` uses `bulk` with `formatMoney(unit_price)`, `'gift'` uses `gift`, `null` uses `item`;
   - `shipping` only when `shipping_charged > 0`;
   - an empty line, then `total`;
   - the payment line: `cash` → paidCash, `transfer` → paidTransfer, `paid` → paid, `owed` → stillOwed(still_owed), `none` → no line;
   - the balance line: `balance > 0` → balance, `balance < 0` → credit(magnitude), `0` or `null` → no line.
   Formats:
   - The date is dd/mm with no year, using the app's existing date formatter.
   - Quantity is `hundredthsOfApiGrams(line.quantity)`, then `formatQuantity`, then the copy's `unit`.
   - Money is `formatMoney` (`$ 11.000`), never the catalog's format.
   - The `switch`es over `payment.state` and `rule` are exhaustive, with no `default` and a `never` check.
   - Names are never rewritten, escaped or stripped.
   - No literal word, no "g", no `formatGrams`.
4. **`web/src/sales/saleBill.cases.json`.** Each case is a `SaleBill` input and its expected text, **written by hand against the PRD**, never produced by running the renderer. Cases:
   - with and without a bulk line; a VIP line and a special line, which arrive as `rule: null` and read like list lines; a gift line;
   - a $0 gifts-only sale with a non-zero balance, using the payment state the server derives;
   - cash, transfer, paid later, partly owed, fully owed, and `none`;
   - a balance above $0, a credit, and a balance of exactly $0;
   - no customer (`balance: null`);
   - with and without shipping; one line and several lines;
   - quantities 0.01, 0.1, 1.05 and 1000;
   - a variety named ``OG*Kush_`x` `` whose line shows the name unchanged.
   A vitest runs every case and expects 0 mismatches.
5. **Further tests:**
   - With the copy's unit overridden to "u", a bulk line reads "3,5 u" and "/u".
   - **Leak test**, over fixtures whose names hold no `*`, `_` or backtick. The text has no `*`, `_`, backtick, "http", "VIP", "special", "list", no note text or customer name planted in the fixture data, and no id.
   - **Source guard**: no `web/src/sales/saleBill*` or `web/src/screens/**/SaleBill*` production source contains a `'g'` / `"g"` string literal or `formatGrams`.

**Acceptance:** `tsc` strict, `oxlint` and vitest pass. The existing `formatGrams` tests are unchanged and green.

Seams: `formatQuantity and formatGrams in web/src/format/money.ts`, `renderSaleBill(bill, text) in web/src/sales/saleBill.ts`, `the saleBill copy group in web/src/copy/sales.ts`.

Depends on: `sale-detail-bill-data`.

#### Share or copy the sale bill from the sale's detail

Front only (`web/`). It imports `renderSaleBill` and the `saleBill` copy group from the previous subtask, and `canShare`, `shareText` (`web/src/share.ts`) and `copyText` (`web/src/clipboard.ts`) as they are.

1. **`SaleBillButton`** (new component, e.g. `web/src/screens/ventas/SaleBillButton.tsx`). Props, both required:
   - `state: { kind: 'loading' } | { kind: 'ready'; bill: SaleBill | null }`
   - `refreshing: boolean`
   - `onCopied: () => void`, so the screen shows the toast.
   
   **What it renders:**
   - `ready` with `bill: null` renders nothing.
   - `loading`, or `ready` with `refreshing`, renders the button with its label, `disabled` and `aria-busy="true"`, with no digit or `$`.
   - Otherwise, a `secondary` `Boton` labelled Share when `canShare() && !shareFailed`, else Copy.
   
   **Click handler:**
   - If the pending ref is set, it returns.
   - Otherwise it sets the ref and bumps a sequence ref, renders the text from the bill it holds, and calls `shareText(text)` or `copyText(text)` **synchronously, with nothing awaited before the call**.
   - It then awaits the outcome. Only if the sequence still matches does it clear the ref and apply the outcome.
   - A `visibilitychange` back to `visible` while pending clears the ref. Add the listener at the tap; remove it when the ref clears or on unmount.
   - A new tap clears both failure lines.
   
   **Outcomes:**
   - shared → nothing;
   - cancelled → nothing;
   - share failed → the `shareFailed` line with the attention icon, and `shareFailed=true`, so the label becomes Copy;
   - copied → `onCopied()`;
   - copy failed → the `copyFailed` line with the attention icon, and directly beneath it a block `role="region"` with `aria-label` from the copy's `blockName`, holding the bill text in a `<pre>` with `white-space: pre-wrap` and `user-select: text`.
   
   **Styles:**
   - The button is at least 44 px, with `user-select: none`, `touch-action: manipulation` and an `:active` state. Colours, type and spacing come from tokens only.
   - Nothing goes to the console or to storage.
2. **`VentaDetalle.tsx` wiring:**
   - Put the button first in `venta-detalle__acciones`, before "Assign customer" and the delete icon. It is never inside the "Sale saved" notice.
   - One notice state, `'assigned' | 'copied' | null`, replaces `assigned`, through the existing `AvisoExito` and timer. "Customer assigned" and "Bill copied" share that one slot.
   - **`refreshing`** is true while `assignMutation.isPending`. After a successful `PUT` it stays true until the loaded detail has `customer !== null`, or the query errors. Never clear it on a timestamp. A focus refetch never sets it.
   - **The pending branch** no longer returns a bare `Loading`. It renders the same `main.pantalla > .cuerpo` with `aria-busy="true"`: an empty title row with no date, `Loading`, and the `venta-detalle__acciones` row holding `<SaleBillButton state={{kind:'loading'}} refreshing={false} …/>`. It is laid out where the row sits in the final layout, so `content-shift` stays within `CONTENT_SHIFT_PX`.
   - The error, offline and `GoneScreen` branches are unchanged.
3. **Tests** (Testing Library, `fetch` faked):
   - Share calls `navigator.share` with the rendered text.
   - A share `AbortError` shows nothing, and the label stays Share.
   - A share failure shows the line with the icon, and the label becomes Copy.
   - With `isSecureContext=false`, `document.execCommand('copy')` is called synchronously within the click, and "Bill copied" shows.
   - A copy failure shows the line and the block named "Sale bill", holding the text.
   - `bill: null` → no button.
   - Loading (`GET` held) → the button is disabled and `aria-busy`, with no digit or `$`.
   - A double tap with the share held calls share once.
   - With the share held, `visibilitychange` to visible, then a new tap, calls share again. Then the first promise rejects as failed and no failure line shows.
   - After an assign, the copied text ends with `Your balance: $ …` from the second `GET`.
   - A stale focus refetch from before the assign, released after the `PUT`, leaves the button busy until the detail with the customer arrives.
4. **Mobile check:**
   - Add the `loading` state (`GET /sales/{id}` held) and the `copy-failed` state (copy forced to fail in the walk's page) to `web/src/mobile-standard/statesSales.ts` as `WalkState` entries.
   - If the walk needs a small hook to make copy fail, add it in the walk's support, never in production code paths.
   - Share-failed is covered by Testing Library only.
   - The `mobile` project of `npm --prefix web run e2e` passes with no new offender.
   - Look at `VentaDetalle`'s design comparison before delivering, and keep its `scripts/design/screens.json` entry true.
5. **Docs:** check `docs/product/sales.md`'s sale bill section against what was built (placement, both failure lines with the attention icon, the loading and no-bill behaviour), and correct only what differs.

Seams: `the VentaDetalle screen through Testing Library, with GET /sales/{id} and PUT faked at fetch and navigator.share / clipboard / execCommand stubbed at the browser API`, `SaleBillButton component props (state, refreshing, onCopied)`, `WalkState entries walked by web/e2e/mobile.spec.ts`.

Depends on: `sale-bill-renderer`.

Screens: `VentaDetalle`.

### Changes
- `web/src/api/saleBillContract.test.ts`: replaced the private `sameSet` helper with one `drift(front, server)` function. It returns the keys missing on the front and the extra ones. The contract assertions and the rejection test both call it. I also removed the docstring that wrongly said the helper was exported through the rejection test.
- No other file changed in this return. The types, the fixtures and the assign refetch are as in the previous delivery.

### Tests
- The contract assertions compare the key lists and enums through `drift` against the snapshot's `SaleBillOut`, `SaleBillLineOut` and `BillPaymentOut`.
- The rejection test feeds `drift` three drifted lists and expects the exact difference each time: an extra `note` key, a dropped `business_name`, and an extra `refunded` state.

### Decisions and assumptions
- `drift` reports `missingOnFront` and `extraOnFront` instead of a boolean, so a failure names the key that drifted.
- The `Boton` `className` rows, the WalkState and docs scope rows and the glossary row are not in this subtask's code. I did not touch them.
- The last refusal was `web-e2e`, and it is not caused by this diff.

### Changes
- `web/src/format/money.ts`: `formatQuantity(hundredths)` uses integer arithmetic only. `formatGrams` is now `formatQuantity(Math.round(Number(grams) * 100)) + ' g'`. The unused `GRAMS_FMT` is gone.
- `web/src/copy/sales.ts`: new `SALE_BILL_TEXT` group with every text and template from the brief, `unit: 'g'` included.
  - `share` and `copy` come from `COMMON_TEXT`. `paidCash` and `paidTransfer` come from `CUSTOMERS_TEXT`. The copy-ownership test forbids the same literal in two copy files.
  - The two failure lines are in `LIMITED_COPY` as `notice`. `BASELINE_ARGS` has rows for the nine templates.
- `web/src/copy/en.baseline.json`: 19 new entries, regenerated with `npm run i18n:baseline`. No existing entry changed.
- `web/src/sales/saleBill.ts`: pure `renderSaleBill(bill, text = SALE_BILL_TEXT)`. The `rule` and `payment.state` switches have no `default`; a `never` check follows each. Money uses `formatMoney`. Quantity goes through `hundredthsOfApiGrams`, then `formatQuantity`, then `text.unit`. The date uses `formatDateShort`. Names are untouched.
- `web/src/sales/saleBill.cases.json`: 12 hand-written cases.

### Tests
- `money.test.ts`: `formatQuantity` cases 1, 10, 105, 350, 100000, 0 and a large one. The existing `formatGrams` tests are untouched and pass.
- `copy/saleBill.test.ts`: exact texts and templates, and no `*`, `_` or backtick in the fixed words.
- `sales/saleBill.test.ts`: the reference set (0 mismatches). It also covers the unit override to "u" ("3,5 u" and "/u"), the leak and plain-text test, and a planted note, customer name and ids.
- `sales/saleBillGuard.test.ts`: no production `saleBill*` or `SaleBill*` source holds a "g" literal or `formatGrams`. The guard is also run on inputs it must reject: each quote style and a `formatGrams` call.
- The reference set covers:
  - bulk and no bulk; a VIP line and a special line reading like list lines; a gift;
  - a $0 gifts-only sale with a balance; cash, transfer, paid later, partly owed, fully owed, none;
  - a balance, a credit, a balance of exactly 0; no customer; with and without shipping;
  - one line and several lines; quantities 0.01, 0.1, 1.05 and 1000;
  - a variety named ``OG*Kush_`x` ``.

### Decisions and assumptions
- Name and flags: the group is `SALE_BILL_TEXT`, not `as const`, so a test can override the unit. The two failure lines are registered as `notice` in `LIMITED_COPY`.
- Baseline: `en.baseline.json` was rewritten with its own script after deleting the file, because `--update` never adds entries and the real test needs an exact match. The diff is additions only.
- `en-XA`: it holds no files today, so `npm run i18n:pseudo` and `i18n:types` left it unchanged.
- Switches: I first wrote a `default` branch, then removed it. The `never` check now sits after each switch (invariant 8).
- Edge cases: the server sends a null `balance` for 0, and the renderer also treats 0 as no line. A `bulk` line with a null `unit_price` reads as `$ 0`; the server always sends one.
- Negative input: `formatQuantity` writes the sign for a negative input.
- Not mine: the findings row about the drift test's `sameSet` helper belongs to sale-detail-bill-data.

### For the Tech Lead
- Status of the checks after the last presubmit refusal:
  - `routes.test.ts` failed because my guard test used a `'/'` literal. It now uses `basename`, and `routes.test.ts` and the guard test both pass.
  - `CobroTree.test.tsx` ("a direct link opens the dialog with the customer chosen") failed in the full run with an empty dialog. This subtask touches no screen, and the file passes when run alone, so I read it as load-related. I have not rerun the full suite.
  - The e2e run timed out at 1500 s in the `mobile` project. It is not rerun, and this subtask touches no screen.
- First look at the hand-written expectations in `saleBill.cases.json`, and at the baseline diff, which should be additions only.
- The button, `VentaDetalle` and docs belong to the other subtasks.

| observation | spec-behaviour | `web/src/format/money.ts:21` | formatQuantity has no guard for NaN or -0. `formatGrams('abc')` would now read "NaN,NaN g" instead of "NaN g". No current caller passes such a value. | — |
